Title: A Brief Account of the Gracious Dealings of God, with a Poor, Sinful, Unworthy Creature, in Three Parts
Creator: Anne Dutton (title page: 'By A. D.'; the archive.org record gives the name)
Publisher: London: printed by J. Hart in Popping's Court, Fleet-Street, and sold by J. Lewis in Pater-noster-Row (title page)
Publication Date: 1750 (title page, OCR line 77)
Rights: Public Domain (pd-us-by-date: published 1750); the archive.org record carries no rights field, so the imprint date is the basis
Language: eng
Source: archive.org item bim_eighteenth-century_a-brief-account-of-the-g_dutton-anne_1750, full text from the item's own djvu.txt (bim_eighteenth-century_a-brief-account-of-the-g_dutton-anne_1750_djvu.txt). Provenance note: Extracted unaltered: the item's own djvu.txt is reproduced whole after this header; nothing was stripped, normalized or corrected. Rights basis: published 1750, out of US copyright by date. OCR quality: mostly legible, with the long s kept; the title page is noisy and OCR lines 86-112 are junk characters, and catchwords and margins are mixed into the text. Word count of the host text: 139,335. Line numbers cited in this header are lines of the host OCR; add 12 for this file's own line numbers.
Content note: The three parts of her own account, with her appendix and a letter on printing by a woman. General title page at OCR lines 1-77; her note to the reader at 116-175, saying Parts I and II were printed 'some Years ago'. Part I: title page at 210-241, heading at 242, her preface at 306. Part II: title page from 4159 (its imprint reads 1743 at 4218). Part III: title page from 13871, text from 14005, with her numbered list of her tracts, I-XXI, at about 18592-18731. Appendix from 19469, continuing the list as XXII-XXXIII at 21879-21927. Errata at 23455. The letter 'To such of the Servants of Christ, who may have any Scruple about the Lawfulness of Printing any Thing written by a Woman': title at 23481-23547, text from 23554 to the end of the file at 24020. Her own letters, and friends' letters of 1741, are quoted inside Parts II and III.

Dear Reader, 


| HE Firſt and Second Parts of my 
Brief Account, were printed ſome 
Years ago, as appears by their Date. And 
the Third Part, which is now added, per- 
haps might not have been printed till after 
my Deceaſe, but for ſpecial Reaſons which 
inducd me to it. I have fincerely aim d in 
the Whole, ſo far as I know my own Heart, 
at the Glory of Gop, and the Good of 
Souls: And not at my own Honour, that 
any Man ſhould think of me, more highly 
WW then is meet: Or any otherwiſe, than as a 
chief Sinner, on whom the Lord bath 
ſhewed great Mercy. I defire forever to 
be deeply humbled for my Unworthineſs 
and Vileneſs, and that Gop's Free Grace 
alone, may be highly and eternally exalted, 
in the Whole of my Salvation, in my preſent 
and future Bliſs. Ob, Not unto me, Not 1 
unto me, unto vile unworthy me; but unto |

Y Dejfign in the following Sheets, was to 
bear a Teſtimony, as one of God's 
itneſſes, to the exceeding Riches of his Grace, 
the Salvation of my Soul, But yet, when I 
began to think about it, (having been requeſt- 
to engage in this Work) I could not, for ſome 
we, find my Heart inclined hereto: As being 
der a prevailing Senſe of my o“ Weakneſs, to 
any Thing that might tend to the Advance- 
it of God's Free-Grace, and the Edification 
his People. So that thou hadſt never had the 
lowing Narrative, if the Lord himſelf had not 
ourag'd me to attempt this Work. And he 
el drew my Heart, as being one of his Wil- 
ſes, to bear this Teſtimony for Him, and his 
py of javing poor Sinners, of whom I am 
ef. 1 ſaw a great Glory in thoſe Words, 
v. XIv. 1. And I looked, and lo, a Lamb 
od on the Mount Sion; and with him an 
dred forty and four Thouſand, having his 
her's Name written in their Forcheads. 
as one of the Lamb's Company, I was mad? 
ling to bear my weak Teſtimony to the All- 
ciency of God's Free-Grace, and the Inſuffi- 
cy of every Thing elſe for the Salvation of a 
Sinner. And thus to look forth into the 
, 4 having the Father's Name written on 
Verecbead : i. e. As living Witneſs of that

1. Let the dear Saints be cautions, when

rances; How they draw out Ways, and fix

their Deſires, in granting them, not thoſe

they molt earneſtly i and expe, but in 
others, which for his Glory and their Good, 
are far, far, better. Tis true, our good God, 


were all at once. And an unthought of Inſtrument, 
my dear Huſband, the Lord employ'd, to diſperſe 
them in America. e MES | : 

But my farther Uſefulneſs, which the Lord alſo 
intended, did particularly reſpe& this poor little 
Church, for which I all along was much concerned. 
The Promiſes which the Lord gave me after my 
Huſband's Departure, chiefly reſpected their Proſ- 
perity, Which I moſt earneſtly ſought for. The 
Trials I met with relating to the Church, occaſion'd 
by my Huſband's long Stay, and the Delay of the An- 
ſwer of my Prayers, were very great, a Wilderneſs 
indeed ; into which the Lord brought, and in ſome 
Senſe allur'd me: And fitly was this my State re- 
ſembled by the Buſh on Fire, and not conſumed. 
But when the Time of the Promiſe reſpeRing the 
Church, drew nigh, the Lord in boundleſs Mercy 
and infinite Faithfulneſs, rais'd up a Deliverer, and 
wrought Deliverance, by ſending the Miniſter be 
had provided ; and whom he gave to be our Paſtor. 
—— Thus in both Reſpects, was I ſatisfy' d with the 
Lord's promis'd Goodneſs, and my Trials in both, 
were bleſt for my Advantage,

be with me, and my Gop ſtill, notwithſtanding 
my Unprofitableneſs. Loveſ thou ME? 
Feed my Lambs, Joh. xxi, 15. By this my 
dear Lord calPd for this little Piece to come out,
